<issue_description>
Allow the exporting of grades per assignment id
The main issue I have with the exporting of grades (csv export) is that it is extremely sluggish exporting the entire gradebook (5min+), especially in cases where there may only be one or two grade changes for a given assignment.

My suggestion is to allow the exporting of grades to individual files per assignment id, if an assignment id is specified, otherwise export the entire gradebook.

A custom plugin can be created to achieve this, but I think it may be better to support this.
@jhamrick what do you think?

This is also something that is specific to the use case of a large number of students.
</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze the issue description:

1. The main problem is that exporting grades (CSV export) is extremely sluggish - taking 5+ minutes

- CSV export functionality needs modification

- Minimal changes to existing functionality
- Backward compatibility
- Clear API/interface for specifying assignment ID
- Proper error handling for invalid assignment IDs</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.3 Problem Explanation
The issue is about performance optimization for grade exporting in nbgrader. Currently, when instructors want to export grades to CSV, the entire gradebook is exported regardless of whether they only need grades for a specific assignment. This becomes problematic with large numbers of students, taking 5+ minutes to complete. The solution is to add functionality that allows exporting grades for individual assignments by ID, significantly improving performance when only specific assignment data is needed.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Have a large gradebook with many students and assignments
2. Attempt to export grades via CSV export functionality
3. Observe slow performance (5+ minutes) even when only needing data for one assignment

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Maintain backward compatibility - existing export functionality should remain unchanged
- Add optional assignment ID parameter to export function
- Validate assignment ID existence before processing
- Optimize database queries to fetch only relevant data
- Provide clear error messages for invalid assignment IDs

    14	
    15	### Instructor toolbar extension for Jupyter notebooks
    16	The nbgrader toolbar extension for Jupyter notebooks guides the instructor through

    18

    20

    22	
    23	The formgrader extension for the Jupyter notebook allows instructors to use
    24	the core functionality of nbgrader---generating the student version of an
    25	assignment, releasing assignments to students, collecting assignments,
    26	autograding submissions, and manually grading submissions.
    27	
    28	![Formgrader extension](nbgrader/docs/source/user_guide/images/formgrader.gif "Formgrader extension")
    29	
    30	### Student assignment list extension for Jupyter notebooks
    31	Using the assignment list extension, students may conveniently view, fetch,
    32	submit, and validate their assignments.
    33	
    34	![nbgrader assignment list](nbgrader/docs/source/user_guide/images/student_assignment.gif "nbgrader assignment list")
    35

    37	
    38	[Command line tools](https://nbgrader.readthedocs.io/en/latest/command_line_tools/index.html)
    39	offer an efficient way for the instructor to generate, assign, release, collect,

     1	from traitlets import Unicode
     2	
     3	from .base import BasePlugin
     4	from ..api import MissingEntry
     5	
     6	
     7	class ExportPlugin(BasePlugin):
     8	    """Base class for export plugins."""
     9	
    10	    to = Unicode("", help="destination to export to").tag(config=True)
    11	
    12	    def export(self, gradebook):
    13	        """Export grades to another format.
    14	
    15	        This method MUST be implemented by subclasses. Users should be able to
    16	        pass the ``--to`` flag on the command line, which will set the
    17	        ``self.to`` variable. By default, this variable will be an empty string,
    18	        which allows you to specify whatever default you would like.
    19	
    20	        Arguments
    21	        ---------
    22	        gradebook: :class:`nbgrader.api.Gradebook`
    23	            An instance of the gradebook
    24	
    25	        """
    26	        raise NotImplementedError
    27	
    28	
    29	class CsvExportPlugin(ExportPlugin):
    30	    """CSV exporter plugin."""
    31	
    32	    def export(self, gradebook):
    33	        if self.to == "":
    34	            dest = "grades.csv"
    35	        else:
    36	            dest = self.to
    37	
    38	        self.log.info("Exporting grades to %s", dest)
    39	
    40	        fh = open(dest, "w")
    41	        keys = [
    42	            "assignment",
    43	            "duedate",
    44	            "timestamp",
    45	            "student_id",
    46	            "last_name",
    47	            "first_name",
    48	            "email",
    49	            "raw_score",
    50	            "late_submission_penalty",
    51	            "score",
    52	            "max_score"
    53	        ]
    54	        fh.write(",".join(keys) + "\n")
    55	        fmt = ",".join(["{" + x + "}" for x in keys]) + "\n"
    56	
    57	        # Loop over each assignment in the database
    58	        for assignment in gradebook.assignments:
    59	
    60	            # Loop over each student in the database
    61	            for student in gradebook.students:
    62	
    63	                # Create a dictionary that will store information about this
    64	                # student's submitted assignment
    65	                score = {}
    66	                score['assignment'] = assignment.name
    67	                score['duedate'] = assignment.duedate
    68	                score['student_id'] = student.id
    69	                score['last_name'] = student.last_name
    70	                score['first_name'] = student.first_name
    71	                score['email'] = student.email
    72	                score['max_score'] = assignment.max_score
    73	
    74	                # Try to find the submission in the database. If it doesn't
    75	                # exist, the `MissingEntry` exception will be raised, which
    76	                # means the student didn't submit anything, so we assign them a
    77	                # score of zero.
    78	                try:
    79	                    submission = gradebook.find_submission(
    80	                        assignment.name, student.id)
    81	                except MissingEntry:
    82	                    score['timestamp'] = ''
    83	                    score['raw_score'] = 0.0
    84	                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = 0.0
    85	                    score['score'] = 0.0
    86	                else:
    87	                    penalty = submission.late_submission_penalty
    88	                    score['timestamp'] = submission.timestamp
    89	                    score['raw_score'] = submission.score
    90	                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = penalty
    91	                    score['score'] = max(0.0, submission.score - penalty)
    92	
    93	                for key in score:
    94	                    if score[key] is None:
    95	                        score[key] = ''
    96	                    if not isinstance(score[key], str):
    97	                        score[key] = str(score[key])
    98	
    99	                fh.write(fmt.format(**score))
   100	
   101	        fh.close()
   102

Export information from the database to another format.

Options
-------

Arguments that take values are actually convenience aliases to full
Configurables, whose aliases are listed on the help line. For more information
on full configurables, see '--help-all'.

--log-level=<Enum> (Application.log_level)
    Default: 30
    Choices: (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 'DEBUG', 'INFO', 'WARN', 'ERROR', 'CRITICAL')
    Set the log level by value or name.
--db=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.db_url)
    Default: ''
    URL to the database. Defaults to sqlite:///<root>/gradebook.db, where <root>
    is another configurable variable.
--to=<Unicode> (ExportPlugin.to)
    Default: ''
    destination to export to
--exporter=<Type> (ExportApp.plugin_class)
    Default: 'nbgrader.plugins.export.CsvExportPlugin'
    The plugin class for exporting the grades.

Class parameters
----------------

Parameters are set from command-line arguments of the form:
`--Class.trait=value`. This line is evaluated in Python, so simple expressions
are allowed, e.g.:: `--C.a='range(3)'` For setting C.a=[0,1,2].

NbGrader options
----------------
--NbGrader.answer_yes=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Answer yes to any prompts.
--NbGrader.config_file=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    Full path of a config file.
--NbGrader.config_file_name=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    Specify a config file to load.
--NbGrader.generate_config=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Generate default config file.
--NbGrader.log_datefmt=<Unicode>
    Default: '%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S'
    The date format used by logging formatters for %(asctime)s
--NbGrader.log_format=<Unicode>
    Default: '[%(name)s]%(highlevel)s %(message)s'
    The Logging format template
--NbGrader.log_level=<Enum>
    Default: 30
    Choices: (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 'DEBUG', 'INFO', 'WARN', 'ERROR', 'CRITICAL')
    Set the log level by value or name.
--NbGrader.logfile=<Unicode>
    Default: '.nbgrader.log'
    Name of the logfile to log to.

CourseDirectory options
-----------------------
--CourseDirectory.assignment_id=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    The assignment name. This MUST be specified, either by setting the config
    option, passing an argument on the command line, or using the --assignment
    option on the command line.
--CourseDirectory.autograded_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'autograded'
    The name of the directory that contains assignment submissions after they
    have been autograded. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in
    the `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.db_assignments=<List>
    Default: []
    A list of assignments that will be created in the database. Each item in the
    list should be a dictionary with the following keys:
        - name
        - duedate (optional)
    The values will be stored in the database. Please see the API documentation
    on the `Assignment` database model for details on these fields.
--CourseDirectory.db_students=<List>
    Default: []
    A list of student that will be created in the database. Each item in the
    list should be a dictionary with the following keys:
        - id
        - first_name (optional)
        - last_name (optional)
        - email (optional)
    The values will be stored in the database. Please see the API documentation
    on the `Student` database model for details on these fields.
--CourseDirectory.db_url=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    URL to the database. Defaults to sqlite:///<root>/gradebook.db, where <root>
    is another configurable variable.
--CourseDirectory.directory_structure=<Unicode>
    Default: '{nbgrader_step}/{student_id}/{assignment_id}'
    Format string for the directory structure that nbgrader works over during
    the grading process. This MUST contain named keys for 'nbgrader_step',
    'student_id', and 'assignment_id'. It SHOULD NOT contain a key for
    'notebook_id', as this will be automatically joined with the rest of the
    path.
--CourseDirectory.feedback_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'feedback'
    The name of the directory that contains assignment feedback after grading
    has been completed. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in the
    `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.ignore=<List>
    Default: ['.ipynb_checkpoints', '*.pyc', '__pycache__']
    List of file names or file globs to be ignored when copying directories.
--CourseDirectory.notebook_id=<Unicode>
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match notebook names, excluding the '.ipynb' extension. This
    can be changed to filter by notebook.
--CourseDirectory.release_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'release'
    The name of the directory that contains the version of the assignment that
    will be released to students. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step`
    variable in the `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.root=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    The root directory for the course files (that includes the `source`,
    `release`, `submitted`, `autograded`, etc. directories). Defaults to the
    current working directory.
--CourseDirectory.source_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'source'
    The name of the directory that contains the master/instructor version of
    assignments. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in the
    `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.student_id=<Unicode>
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match student IDs. This can be changed to filter by student.
    Note: this is always changed to '.' when running `nbgrader assign`, as the
    assign step doesn't have any student ID associated with it.
    If the ID is purely numeric and you are passing it as a flag on the command
    line, you will need to escape the quotes in order to have it detected as a
    string, for example `--student=""12345""`. See:
        https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader/issues/743
    for more details.
--CourseDirectory.submitted_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'submitted'
    The name of the directory that contains assignments that have been submitted
    by students for grading. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in
    the `directory_structure` config option.

--ExportApp.answer_yes=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Answer yes to any prompts.

--ExportApp.log_level=<Enum>
    Default: 30
    Choices: (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 'DEBUG', 'INFO', 'WARN', 'ERROR', 'CRITICAL')
    Set the log level by value or name.
--ExportApp.logfile=<Unicode>
    Default: '.nbgrader.log'
    Name of the logfile to log to.
--ExportApp.plugin_class=<Type>
    Default: 'nbgrader.plugins.export.CsvExportPlugin'
    The plugin class for exporting the grades.

ExportPlugin options
--------------------
--ExportPlugin.to=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    destination to export to

Examples
--------

    The default is to export to a file called "grades.csv", i.e.:

        nbgrader export

    You can customize the filename with the --to flag:

        nbgrader export --to mygrades.csv

    To change the export type, you will need a class that inherits from
    nbgrader.plugins.ExportPlugin. If your exporter is named
    `MyCustomExporter` and is saved in the file `myexporter.py`, then:

        nbgrader export --exporter=myexporter.MyCustomExporter

1. The current export functionality exports ALL assignments for ALL students 
2. There's no way to filter by assignment ID
3. The CourseDirectory has an `assignment_id` field that could be leveraged
4. We need to add a way to specify which assignment to export

"""Reproduction script to test current export behavior and planned enhancement."""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
from nbgrader.api import Gradebook
from nbgrader.apps.exportapp import ExportApp
from nbgrader.plugins.export import CsvExportPlugin

def setup_test_environment():
    """Create a temporary directory with test data."""
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    print(f"Created temp directory: {temp_dir}")
    
    # Create directory structure
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2")) 
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps1"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps2"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps1"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps2"))
    
    # Create simple notebook files
    notebook_content = '''{
 "cells": [
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "execution_count": null,
   "metadata": {},
   "outputs": [],
   "source": [
    "# Simple test notebook\\n",
    "print(\\"Hello World\\")"
   ]
  }
 ],
 "metadata": {
  "kernelspec": {
   "display_name": "Python 3",
   "language": "python",
   "name": "python3"
  },
  "nbgrader": {
   "assignment_id": "ps1"
  }
 },
 "nbformat": 4,
 "nbformat_minor": 4
}'''
    
    with open(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb"), "w") as f:
        f.write(notebook_content.replace('"assignment_id": "ps1"', '"assignment_id": "ps1"'))
        
    with open(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb"), "w") as f:
        f.write(notebook_content.replace('"assignment_id": "ps1"', '"assignment_id": "ps2"'))
        
    # Copy to submitted directories
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    
    return temp_dir

def test_current_export_behavior():
    """Test the current export behavior - exports all assignments."""
    print("\n=== Testing Current Export Behavior ===")
    
    temp_dir = setup_test_environment()
    db_url = f"sqlite:///{os.path.join(temp_dir, 'gradebook.db')}"
    
    try:
        # Initialize gradebook
        with Gradebook(db_url) as gb:
            # Add assignments
            gb.add_assignment("ps1")
            gb.add_assignment("ps2")
            
            # Add students
            gb.add_student("student1", first_name="First", last_name="Student1")
            gb.add_student("student2", first_name="Second", last_name="Student2")
            
            print(f"Created {len(gb.assignments)} assignments: {[a.name for a in gb.assignments]}")
            print(f"Created {len(gb.students)} students: {[s.id for s in gb.students]}")
        
        # Test current export - should export all assignments
        print("\nTesting full export...")
        export_app = ExportApp(coursedir={"db_url": db_url})
        export_app.initialize([])
        export_app.start()
        
        if os.path.exists("grades.csv"):
            with open("grades.csv", "r") as f:
                lines = f.readlines()
                print(f"Full export created grades.csv with {len(lines)} lines")
                print("First few lines:")
                for i, line in enumerate(lines[:5]):
                    print(f"  {line.strip()}")
        else:
            print("ERROR: grades.csv was not created")
            
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        os.chdir("/workspace/jupyter__nbgrader__0.5")
        if os.path.exists("grades.csv"):
            os.remove("grades.csv")
        shutil.rmtree(temp_dir)

def test_desired_behavior():
    """Test what we want to achieve - export by assignment ID."""
    print("\n=== Testing Desired Behavior (will fail initially) ===")

            os.remove("grades_ps1.csv")
        shutil.rmtree(temp_dir)

"""Reproduction script to test current export behavior and planned enhancement."""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
from nbgrader.api import Gradebook
from nbgrader.apps.exportapp import ExportApp
from nbgrader.plugins.export import CsvExportPlugin

def setup_test_environment():
    """Create a temporary directory with test data."""
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    print(f"Created temp directory: {temp_dir}")
    
    # Create directory structure
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2")) 
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps1"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps2"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps1"))
    os.makedirs(os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps2"))
    
    # Create simple notebook files
    notebook_content = '''{
 "cells": [
  {
   "cell_type": "code",
   "execution_count": null,
   "metadata": {},
   "outputs": [],
   "source": [
    "# Simple test notebook\\n",
    "print(\\"Hello World\\")"
   ]
  }
 ],
 "metadata": {
  "kernelspec": {
   "display_name": "Python 3",
   "language": "python",
   "name": "python3"
  },
  "nbgrader": {
   "assignment_id": "ps1"
  }
 },
 "nbformat": 4,
 "nbformat_minor": 4
}'''
    
    with open(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb"), "w") as f:
        f.write(notebook_content.replace('"assignment_id": "ps1"', '"assignment_id": "ps1"'))
        
    with open(os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb"), "w") as f:
        f.write(notebook_content.replace('"assignment_id": "ps1"', '"assignment_id": "ps2"'))
        
    # Copy to submitted directories
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student1", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps1", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    shutil.copy(
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "source", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb"),
        os.path.join(temp_dir, "submitted", "student2", "ps2", "problem1.ipynb")
    )
    
    return temp_dir

def test_current_export_behavior():
    """Test the current export behavior - exports all assignments."""
    print("\n=== Testing Current Export Behavior ===")
    
    temp_dir = setup_test_environment()
    db_url = f"sqlite:///{os.path.join(temp_dir, 'gradebook.db')}"
    
    try:
        # Initialize gradebook
        with Gradebook(db_url) as gb:
            # Add assignments
            gb.add_assignment("ps1")
            gb.add_assignment("ps2")
            
            # Add students
            gb.add_student("student1", first_name="First", last_name="Student1")
            gb.add_student("student2", first_name="Second", last_name="Student2")
            
            print(f"Created {len(gb.assignments)} assignments: {[a.name for a in gb.assignments]}")
            print(f"Created {len(gb.students)} students: {[s.id for s in gb.students]}")
        
        # Test current export - should export all assignments
        print("\nTesting full export...")
        export_app = ExportApp(coursedir={"db_url": db_url})
        export_app.initialize([])
        export_app.start()
        
        if os.path.exists("grades.csv"):
            with open("grades.csv", "r") as f:
                lines = f.readlines()
                print(f"Full export created grades.csv with {len(lines)} lines")
                print("First few lines:")
                for i, line in enumerate(lines[:5]):
                    print(f"  {line.strip()}")
        else:
            print("ERROR: grades.csv was not created")
            
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        os.chdir("/workspace/jupyter__nbgrader__0.5")
        if os.path.exists("grades.csv"):
            os.remove("grades.csv")
        shutil.rmtree(temp_dir)

def test_desired_behavior():
    """Test what we want to achieve - export by assignment ID."""
    print("\n=== Testing Desired Behavior (will fail initially) ===")

        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Expected error (feature not implemented yet): {e}")
            
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        os.chdir("/workspace/jupyter__nbgrader__0.5")
        if os.path.exists("grades.csv"):
            os.remove("grades.csv")
        if os.path.exists("grades_ps1.csv"):
            os.remove("grades_ps1.csv")
        shutil.rmtree(temp_dir)

"""Reproduction script to test current export behavior and planned enhancement."""

import os

from nbgrader.api import Gradebook

def setup_test_environment():
    """Create a temporary directory with test data."""

    print(f"Created temp directory: {temp_dir}")
    
    # Create directory structure

def test_current_export_behavior():
    """Test the current export behavior - exports all assignments."""
    print("\n=== Testing Current Export Behavior ===")
    
    temp_dir = setup_test_environment()
    db_url = f"sqlite:///{os.path.join(temp_dir, 'gradebook.db')}"
    
    try:
        # Initialize gradebook
        with Gradebook(db_url) as gb:
            # Add assignments
            gb.add_assignment("ps1")
            gb.add_assignment("ps2")
            
            # Add students
            gb.add_student("student1", first_name="First", last_name="Student1")
            gb.add_student("student2", first_name="Second", last_name="Student2")
            
            print(f"Created {len(gb.assignments)} assignments: {[a.name for a in gb.assignments]}")
            print(f"Created {len(gb.students)} students: {[s.id for s in gb.students]}")
        
        # Test current export - should export all assignments using nbgrader command
        print("\nTesting full export using nbgrader command...")
        
        # Change to temp directory for the test
        old_cwd = os.getcwd()
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        
        # Run nbgrader export command
        cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "nbgrader", "export", "--db", db_url]
        result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True)
        
        print(f"Command exit code: {result.returncode}")
        if result.stdout:
            print(f"STDOUT: {result.stdout}")
        if result.stderr:
            print(f"STDERR: {result.stderr}")
        
        if os.path.exists("grades.csv"):
            with open("grades.csv", "r") as f:
                lines = f.readlines()
                print(f"Full export created grades.csv with {len(lines)} lines")
                print("First few lines:")
                for i, line in enumerate(lines[:5]):
                    print(f"  {line.strip()}")
        else:
            print("ERROR: grades.csv was not created")
            
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        os.chdir("/workspace/jupyter__nbgrader__0.5")
        if os.path.exists(os.path.join(temp_dir, "grades.csv")):
            os.remove(os.path.join(temp_dir, "grades.csv"))
        shutil.rmtree(temp_dir, ignore_errors=True)

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze what needs to be implemented:

1. **Problem**: The current export functionality exports ALL assignments for ALL students, which is slow for large datasets when only one assignment is needed.

2. **Location**: The main files involved are:
   - `nbgrader/apps/exportapp.py` - The main export application
   - `nbgrader/plugins/export.py` - The actual export plugins (CsvExportPlugin)

3. **How the test reproduces the issue**: 
   - Current export creates grades.csv with all assignments
   - Trying to use `--assignment` flag fails because it's not implemented

4. **Best practices to consider**:
   - Maintain backward compatibility - if no assignment specified, export all (current behavior)
   - Add assignment filtering capability
   - Follow existing patterns in the codebase
   - Handle invalid assignment IDs gracefully
   - Update help text appropriately

5. **How to fix**:
   - Add `--assignment` flag to exportapp.py aliases
   - Modify CsvExportPlugin to accept and use assignment filtering
   - Update the export logic to only process specified assignment when provided
   - Generate appropriate filename when assignment is specified (e.g., grades_<assignment_id>.csv)
   - Validate that the specified assignment exists

Looking at the current CsvExportPlugin logic:
- It loops through all assignments (`for assignment in gradebook.assignments`)
- For each assignment, it loops through all students
- We need to modify this to filter assignments when an assignment_id is specified

The approach:
1. In ExportApp, add the assignment alias and pass it to the plugin
2. In CsvExportPlugin, accept an assignment_id parameter
3. Modify the export method to filter assignments based on assignment_id if provided
4. Update the filename generation logic</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The current export functionality in nbgrader exports grades for ALL assignments and ALL students, which becomes extremely slow (5+ minutes) when dealing with large numbers of students. Users often only need grades for a specific assignment, but there's no way to filter the export.

### 5.2 Problem Location  
The issue is primarily in two files:
- `nbgrader/apps/exportapp.py` - Main export application that needs the `--assignment` flag
- `nbgrader/plugins/export.py` - CsvExportPlugin that performs the actual export logic

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
Our test shows that:
1. Current export creates `grades.csv` with all assignments (working correctly)
2. Attempting to use `--assignment ps1` fails with "Unrecognized flag" error
3. We want it to create `grades.csv` (or `grades_ps1.csv`) with only the specified assignment data

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Maintain backward compatibility: no assignment specified = export all (current behavior)  
- Follow existing code patterns and conventions
- Handle invalid assignment IDs gracefully with proper error messages
- Update help/documentation appropriately
- Minimal, focused changes

### 5.5 Solution Approach
1. Add `--assignment` flag to ExportApp aliases
2. Pass assignment information to the export plugin
3. Modify CsvExportPlugin to filter assignments when assignment_id is specified
4. Update filename generation logic for assignment-specific exports
5. Add validation for assignment existence

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

from traitlets import Type, Instance, default
from .baseapp import NbGrader, nbgrader_aliases, nbgrader_flags
from ..plugins import ExportPlugin, CsvExportPlugin
from ..api import Gradebook

aliases = {}
aliases.update(nbgrader_aliases)
aliases.update({
    'to' : 'ExportPlugin.to',
    'exporter': 'ExportApp.plugin_class'

    33	        if self.to == "":
    34	            dest = "grades.csv"
    35	        else:
    36	            dest = self.to
    37	
    38	        self.log.info("Exporting grades to %s", dest)
    39	
    40	        fh = open(dest, "w")
    41	        keys = [

    43	            "duedate",

    45	            "student_id",

    55	        fmt = ",".join(["{" + x + "}" for x in keys]) + "\n"
    56	
    57	        # Loop over each assignment in the database
    58	        for assignment in gradebook.assignments:
    59	
    60	            # Loop over each student in the database
    61	            for student in gradebook.students:
    62	
    63	                # Create a dictionary that will store information about this
    64	                # student's submitted assignment
    65	                score = {}
    66	                score['assignment'] = assignment.name
    67	                score['duedate'] = assignment.duedate
    68	                score['student_id'] = student.id
    69	                score['last_name'] = student.last_name
    70	                score['first_name'] = student.first_name
    71	                score['email'] = student.email
    72	                score['max_score'] = assignment.max_score
    73	
    74	                # Try to find the submission in the database. If it doesn't
    75	                # exist, the `MissingEntry` exception will be raised, which
    76	                # means the student didn't submit anything, so we assign them a
    77	                # score of zero.
    78	                try:
    79	                    submission = gradebook.find_submission(
    80	                        assignment.name, student.id)
    81	                except MissingEntry:
    82	                    score['timestamp'] = ''
    83	                    score['raw_score'] = 0.0
    84	                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = 0.0
    85	                    score['score'] = 0.0
    86	                else:
    87	                    penalty = submission.late_submission_penalty
    88	                    score['timestamp'] = submission.timestamp
    89	                    score['raw_score'] = submission.score
    90	                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = penalty
    91	                    score['score'] = max(0.0, submission.score - penalty)
    92	
    93	                for key in score:
    94	                    if score[key] is None:
    95	                        score[key] = ''
    96	                    if not isinstance(score[key], str):
    97	                        score[key] = str(score[key])
    98

class ExportPlugin(BasePlugin):
    """Base class for export plugins."""

    to = Unicode("", help="destination to export to").tag(config=True)

    def export(self, gradebook):
        """Export grades to another format.

        This method MUST be implemented by subclasses. Users should be able to
        pass the ``--to`` flag on the command line, which will set the
        ``self.to`` variable. By default, this variable will be an empty string,
        which allows you to specify whatever default you would like.

        Arguments
        ---------
        gradebook: :class:`nbgrader.api.Gradebook`
            An instance of the gradebook

        """
        raise NotImplementedError


class CsvExportPlugin(ExportPlugin):
    """CSV exporter plugin."""

    def export(self, gradebook):
        # Get assignment_id from parent course directory
        assignment_id = getattr(self.parent, 'coursedir', None)
        if assignment_id is not None:
            assignment_id = assignment_id.assignment_id
        
        # Determine destination filename
        if self.to == "":
            if assignment_id:
                dest = f"grades_{assignment_id}.csv"
            else:
                dest = "grades.csv"
        else:
            dest = self.to

        self.log.info("Exporting grades to %s", dest)

        fh = open(dest, "w")
        keys = [
            "assignment",
            "duedate",
            "timestamp",
            "student_id",
            "last_name",
            "first_name",
            "email",
            "raw_score",
            "late_submission_penalty",
            "score",
            "max_score"
        ]
        fh.write(",".join(keys) + "\n")
        fmt = ",".join(["{" + x + "}" for x in keys]) + "\n"

        # Determine which assignments to export
        if assignment_id:
            # Validate that the assignment exists
            try:
                assignment = gradebook.find_assignment(assignment_id)
                assignments_to_export = [assignment]
                self.log.info("Exporting grades for assignment: %s", assignment_id)
            except MissingEntry:
                fh.close()
                raise ValueError(f"Assignment '{assignment_id}' not found in gradebook")
        else:
            # Export all assignments
            assignments_to_export = gradebook.assignments
            self.log.info("Exporting grades for all assignments")

        # Loop over selected assignments
        for assignment in assignments_to_export:

            # Loop over each student in the database
            for student in gradebook.students:

                # Create a dictionary that will store information about this
                # student's submitted assignment
                score = {}
                score['assignment'] = assignment.name
                score['duedate'] = assignment.duedate
                score['student_id'] = student.id
                score['last_name'] = student.last_name
                score['first_name'] = student.first_name
                score['email'] = student.email
                score['max_score'] = assignment.max_score

                # Try to find the submission in the database. If it doesn't
                # exist, the `MissingEntry` exception will be raised, which
                # means the student didn't submit anything, so we assign them a
                # score of zero.
                try:
                    submission = gradebook.find_submission(
                        assignment.name, student.id)
                except MissingEntry:
                    score['timestamp'] = ''
                    score['raw_score'] = 0.0
                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = 0.0
                    score['score'] = 0.0
                else:
                    penalty = submission.late_submission_penalty
                    score['timestamp'] = submission.timestamp
                    score['raw_score'] = submission.score
                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = penalty
                    score['score'] = max(0.0, submission.score - penalty)

                for key in score:
                    if score[key] is None:
                        score[key] = ''
                    if not isinstance(score[key], str):
                        score[key] = str(score[key])

                fh.write(fmt.format(**score))

        fh.close()</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>from traitlets import Unicode

class ExportPlugin(BasePlugin):
    """Base class for export plugins."""

        gradebook: :class:`nbgrader.api.Gradebook`
            An instance of the gradebook

        """
        raise NotImplementedError


class CsvExportPlugin(ExportPlugin):
    """CSV exporter plugin."""

    def export(self, gradebook):
        if self.to == "":
            dest = "grades.csv"
        else:
            dest = self.to

        self.log.info("Exporting grades to %s", dest)

        fh = open(dest, "w")
        keys = [
            "assignment",
            "duedate",
            "timestamp",
            "student_id",
            "last_name",
            "first_name",
            "email",
            "raw_score",
            "late_submission_penalty",
            "score",
            "max_score"
        ]
        fh.write(",".join(keys) + "\n")
        fmt = ",".join(["{" + x + "}" for x in keys]) + "\n"

        # Loop over each assignment in the database
        for assignment in gradebook.assignments:

            # Loop over each student in the database
            for student in gradebook.students:

                # Create a dictionary that will store information about this
                # student's submitted assignment
                score = {}
                score['assignment'] = assignment.name
                score['duedate'] = assignment.duedate
                score['student_id'] = student.id
                score['last_name'] = student.last_name
                score['first_name'] = student.first_name
                score['email'] = student.email
                score['max_score'] = assignment.max_score

                # Try to find the submission in the database. If it doesn't
                # exist, the `MissingEntry` exception will be raised, which
                # means the student didn't submit anything, so we assign them a
                # score of zero.
                try:
                    submission = gradebook.find_submission(
                        assignment.name, student.id)
                except MissingEntry:
                    score['timestamp'] = ''
                    score['raw_score'] = 0.0
                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = 0.0
                    score['score'] = 0.0
                else:
                    penalty = submission.late_submission_penalty
                    score['timestamp'] = submission.timestamp
                    score['raw_score'] = submission.score
                    score['late_submission_penalty'] = penalty
                    score['score'] = max(0.0, submission.score - penalty)

                for key in score:
                    if score[key] is None:
                        score[key] = ''
                    if not isinstance(score[key], str):
                        score[key] = str(score[key])

                fh.write(fmt.format(**score))

import os
from nbgrader.api import Gradebook

# Setup test database

    gb.add_assignment('ps2')
    gb.add_student('student1', first_name='First', last_name='Student1')
    gb.add_student('student2', first_name='Second', last_name='Student2')
    print(f'Created {len(gb.assignments)} assignments: {[a.name for a in gb.assignments]}')

Options
-------

Arguments that take values are actually convenience aliases to full
Configurables, whose aliases are listed on the help line. For more information
on full configurables, see '--help-all'.

--debug
    set log level to DEBUG (maximize logging output)
--quiet
    set log level to CRITICAL (minimize logging output)
--log-level=<Enum> (Application.log_level)
    Default: 30
    Choices: (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 'DEBUG', 'INFO', 'WARN', 'ERROR', 'CRITICAL')
    Set the log level by value or name.
--student=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.student_id)
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match student IDs. This can be changed to filter by student.
    Note: this is always changed to '.' when running `nbgrader assign`, as the
    assign step doesn't have any student ID associated with it.
    If the ID is purely numeric and you are passing it as a flag on the command
    line, you will need to escape the quotes in order to have it detected as a
    string, for example `--student=""12345""`. See:
        https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader/issues/743
    for more details.
--assignment=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.assignment_id)
    Default: ''
    The assignment name. This MUST be specified, either by setting the config
    option, passing an argument on the command line, or using the --assignment
    option on the command line.
--notebook=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.notebook_id)
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match notebook names, excluding the '.ipynb' extension. This
    can be changed to filter by notebook.
--db=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.db_url)
    Default: ''
    URL to the database. Defaults to sqlite:///<root>/gradebook.db, where <root>
    is another configurable variable.
--course-dir=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.root)
    Default: ''
    The root directory for the course files (that includes the `source`,
    `release`, `submitted`, `autograded`, etc. directories). Defaults to the
    current working directory.
--to=<Unicode> (ExportPlugin.to)
    Default: ''
    destination to export to
--exporter=<Type> (ExportApp.plugin_class)
    Default: 'nbgrader.plugins.export.CsvExportPlugin'
    The plugin class for exporting the grades.

Class parameters
----------------

Parameters are set from command-line arguments of the form:
`--Class.trait=value`. This line is evaluated in Python, so simple expressions
are allowed, e.g.:: `--C.a='range(3)'` For setting C.a=[0,1,2].

    Default: ''

    option on the command line.

    Default: 'autograded'
    The name of the directory that contains assignment submissions after they
    have been autograded. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in
    the `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.db_assignments=<List>
    Default: []
    A list of assignments that will be created in the database. Each item in the
    list should be a dictionary with the following keys:
        - name
        - duedate (optional)
    The values will be stored in the database. Please see the API documentation
    on the `Assignment` database model for details on these fields.
--CourseDirectory.db_students=<List>
    Default: []
    A list of student that will be created in the database. Each item in the
    list should be a dictionary with the following keys:
        - id
        - first_name (optional)
        - last_name (optional)
        - email (optional)
    The values will be stored in the database. Please see the API documentation
    on the `Student` database model for details on these fields.
--CourseDirectory.db_url=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    URL to the database. Defaults to sqlite:///<root>/gradebook.db, where <root>
    is another configurable variable.
--CourseDirectory.directory_structure=<Unicode>
    Default: '{nbgrader_step}/{student_id}/{assignment_id}'
    Format string for the directory structure that nbgrader works over during
    the grading process. This MUST contain named keys for 'nbgrader_step',
    'student_id', and 'assignment_id'. It SHOULD NOT contain a key for
    'notebook_id', as this will be automatically joined with the rest of the
    path.
--CourseDirectory.feedback_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'feedback'
    The name of the directory that contains assignment feedback after grading
    has been completed. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in the
    `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.ignore=<List>
    Default: ['.ipynb_checkpoints', '*.pyc', '__pycache__']
    List of file names or file globs to be ignored when copying directories.
--CourseDirectory.notebook_id=<Unicode>
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match notebook names, excluding the '.ipynb' extension. This
    can be changed to filter by notebook.
--CourseDirectory.release_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'release'
    The name of the directory that contains the version of the assignment that
    will be released to students. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step`
    variable in the `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.root=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    The root directory for the course files (that includes the `source`,
    `release`, `submitted`, `autograded`, etc. directories). Defaults to the
    current working directory.
--CourseDirectory.source_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'source'
    The name of the directory that contains the master/instructor version of
    assignments. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in the
    `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.student_id=<Unicode>
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match student IDs. This can be changed to filter by student.
    Note: this is always changed to '.' when running `nbgrader assign`, as the
    assign step doesn't have any student ID associated with it.
    If the ID is purely numeric and you are passing it as a flag on the command
    line, you will need to escape the quotes in order to have it detected as a
    string, for example `--student=""12345""`. See:
        https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader/issues/743
    for more details.
--CourseDirectory.submitted_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'submitted'
    The name of the directory that contains assignments that have been submitted
    by students for grading. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in
    the `directory_structure` config option.

ExportPlugin options
--------------------
--ExportPlugin.to=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    destination to export to

Examples
--------

    The default is to export to a file called "grades.csv", i.e.:

        nbgrader export

    You can customize the filename with the --to flag:

        nbgrader export --to mygrades.csv

    To export grades for only a specific assignment, use the --assignment flag:

        nbgrader export --assignment ps1

    This will create a file called "grades_ps1.csv" with only the grades for
    the "ps1" assignment.

    To change the export type, you will need a class that inherits from
    nbgrader.plugins.ExportPlugin. If your exporter is named
    `MyCustomExporter` and is saved in the file `myexporter.py`, then:

        nbgrader export --exporter=myexporter.MyCustomExporter

"""Comprehensive test for assignment-specific export functionality."""

import os
import tempfile
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys

def run_command(cmd, cwd=None):
    """Run a command and return result."""
    result = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=cwd, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, universal_newlines=True)
    return result

def test_assignment_specific_export():
    """Test exporting grades for specific assignments."""
    print("=== Testing Assignment-Specific Export Functionality ===")
    
    # Create temporary directory
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    print(f"Working in: {temp_dir}")
    
    try:
        # Initialize database with assignments and students
        db_url = f"sqlite:///{os.path.join(temp_dir, 'gradebook.db')}"
        
        # Create assignments and students using nbgrader API
        setup_code = f'''
from nbgrader.api import Gradebook
with Gradebook("{db_url}") as gb:
    # Add assignments
    gb.add_assignment("ps1")
    gb.add_assignment("ps2") 
    gb.add_assignment("ps3")
    
    # Add students
    gb.add_student("student1", first_name="First", last_name="Student1")
    gb.add_student("student2", first_name="Second", last_name="Student2")
    
    print(f"Created {{len(gb.assignments)}} assignments: {{[a.name for a in gb.assignments]}}")
    print(f"Created {{len(gb.students)}} students: {{[s.id for s in gb.students]}}")
'''
        
        result = run_command([sys.executable, "-c", setup_code], cwd=temp_dir)
        print("Database setup output:", result.stdout.strip())
        if result.stderr:
            print("Database setup errors:", result.stderr.strip())
        
        # Test 1: Full export (backward compatibility)
        print("\n--- Test 1: Full export (backward compatibility) ---")
        result = run_command([sys.executable, "-m", "nbgrader", "export", "--db", db_url], cwd=temp_dir)
        print(f"Exit code: {result.returncode}")
        if result.stderr and "WARNING" not in result.stderr:
            print("STDERR:", result.stderr.strip())
            
        if os.path.exists(os.path.join(temp_dir, "grades.csv")):
            with open(os.path.join(temp_dir, "grades.csv"), "r") as f:
                lines = f.readlines()
                print(f"✓ Created grades.csv with {len(lines)} lines")
                # Should have 6 data rows (3 assignments × 2 students) + 1 header
                assert len(lines) == 7, f"Expected 7 lines, got {len(lines)}"
                print("✓ Full export contains correct number of rows")
        else:
            print("✗ grades.csv was not created")
            return False
            
        # Test 2: Assignment-specific export
        print("\n--- Test 2: Assignment-specific export ---")
        result = run_command([sys.executable, "-m", "nbgrader", "export", "--db", db_url, "--assignment", "ps1"], cwd=temp_dir)
        print(f"Exit code: {result.returncode}")
        if result.stderr and "WARNING" not in result.stderr:
            print("STDERR:", result.stderr.strip())
            
        expected_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, "grades_ps1.csv")
        if os.path.exists(expected_file):
            with open(expected_file, "r") as f:
                lines = f.readlines()
                print(f"✓ Created grades_ps1.csv with {len(lines)} lines")
                # Should have 2 data rows (1 assignment × 2 students) + 1 header
                assert len(lines) == 3, f"Expected 3 lines, got {len(lines)}"
                # Check that all rows are for ps1
                for line in lines[1:]:  # Skip header
                    assert line.startswith("ps1,"), f"Row should start with 'ps1,' but got: {line[:10]}"
                print("✓ Assignment-specific export contains correct assignment only")
        else:
            print("✗ grades_ps1.csv was not created")
            return False
            
        # Test 3: Assignment-specific export with custom filename
        print("\n--- Test 3: Assignment-specific export with custom filename ---")
        result = run_command([sys.executable, "-m", "nbgrader", "export", "--db", db_url, "--assignment", "ps2", "--to", "custom_ps2.csv"], cwd=temp_dir)
        print(f"Exit code: {result.returncode}")
        
        expected_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, "custom_ps2.csv")
        if os.path.exists(expected_file):
            with open(expected_file, "r") as f:
                lines = f.readlines()
                print(f"✓ Created custom_ps2.csv with {len(lines)} lines")
                # Should have 2 data rows (1 assignment × 2 students) + 1 header
                assert len(lines) == 3, f"Expected 3 lines, got {len(lines)}"
                # Check that all rows are for ps2
                for line in lines[1:]:  # Skip header
                    assert line.startswith("ps2,"), f"Row should start with 'ps2,' but got: {line[:10]}"
                print("✓ Custom filename export works correctly")
        else:
            print("✗ custom_ps2.csv was not created")
            return False
            
        # Test 4: Error handling for non-existent assignment
        print("\n--- Test 4: Error handling for non-existent assignment ---")
        result = run_command([sys.executable, "-m", "nbgrader", "export", "--db", db_url, "--assignment", "nonexistent"], cwd=temp_dir)
        print(f"Exit code: {result.returncode}")
        
        if result.returncode != 0:
            print("✓ Correctly failed for non-existent assignment")
        else:
            print("✗ Should have failed for non-existent assignment")
            return False
            
        # Test 5: Help text includes new functionality
        print("\n--- Test 5: Help text verification ---")
        result = run_command([sys.executable, "-m", "nbgrader", "export", "--help"], cwd=temp_dir)
        help_text = result.stdout
        
        if "--assignment" in help_text:
            print("✓ --assignment flag is documented in help")
        else:
            print("✗ --assignment flag missing from help")
            return False
            
        if "grades_ps1.csv" in help_text:
            print("✓ Assignment-specific export example is in help")
        else:
            print("✗ Assignment-specific export example missing from help")
            return False
            
        print("\n=== All tests passed! ===")
        return True
        
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        shutil.rmtree(temp_dir, ignore_errors=True)

Options
-------

Arguments that take values are actually convenience aliases to full
Configurables, whose aliases are listed on the help line. For more information
on full configurables, see '--help-all'.

--debug
    set log level to DEBUG (maximize logging output)
--quiet
    set log level to CRITICAL (minimize logging output)
--no-db
    Do not save information into the database.
--no-metadata
    Do not validate or modify cell metatadata.
--create
    Create an entry for the assignment in the database, if one does not already exist.
--force
    Overwrite an assignment/submission if it already exists.
-f
    Overwrite an assignment/submission if it already exists.
--log-level=<Enum> (Application.log_level)
    Default: 30
    Choices: (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 'DEBUG', 'INFO', 'WARN', 'ERROR', 'CRITICAL')
    Set the log level by value or name.
--assignment=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.assignment_id)
    Default: ''
    The assignment name. This MUST be specified, either by setting the config
    option, passing an argument on the command line, or using the --assignment
    option on the command line.
--notebook=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.notebook_id)
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match notebook names, excluding the '.ipynb' extension. This
    can be changed to filter by notebook.
--db=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.db_url)
    Default: ''
    URL to the database. Defaults to sqlite:///<root>/gradebook.db, where <root>
    is another configurable variable.
--course-dir=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.root)
    Default: ''
    The root directory for the course files (that includes the `source`,
    `release`, `submitted`, `autograded`, etc. directories). Defaults to the
    current working directory.

are allowed, e.g.:: `--C.a='range(3)'` For setting C.a=[0,1,2].

CourseDirectory options
-----------------------
--CourseDirectory.assignment_id=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    The assignment name. This MUST be specified, either by setting the config
    option, passing an argument on the command line, or using the --assignment
    option on the command line.
--CourseDirectory.autograded_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'autograded'
    The name of the directory that contains assignment submissions after they
    have been autograded. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in
    the `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.db_assignments=<List>
    Default: []
    A list of assignments that will be created in the database. Each item in the
    list should be a dictionary with the following keys:
        - name
        - duedate (optional)
    The values will be stored in the database. Please see the API documentation
    on the `Assignment` database model for details on these fields.
--CourseDirectory.db_students=<List>
    Default: []
    A list of student that will be created in the database. Each item in the
    list should be a dictionary with the following keys:
        - id
        - first_name (optional)
        - last_name (optional)
        - email (optional)
    The values will be stored in the database. Please see the API documentation
    on the `Student` database model for details on these fields.
--CourseDirectory.db_url=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    URL to the database. Defaults to sqlite:///<root>/gradebook.db, where <root>
    is another configurable variable.
--CourseDirectory.directory_structure=<Unicode>
    Default: '{nbgrader_step}/{student_id}/{assignment_id}'
    Format string for the directory structure that nbgrader works over during
    the grading process. This MUST contain named keys for 'nbgrader_step',
    'student_id', and 'assignment_id'. It SHOULD NOT contain a key for
    'notebook_id', as this will be automatically joined with the rest of the
    path.
--CourseDirectory.feedback_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'feedback'
    The name of the directory that contains assignment feedback after grading
    has been completed. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in the
    `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.ignore=<List>
    Default: ['.ipynb_checkpoints', '*.pyc', '__pycache__']
    List of file names or file globs to be ignored when copying directories.
--CourseDirectory.notebook_id=<Unicode>
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match notebook names, excluding the '.ipynb' extension. This
    can be changed to filter by notebook.
--CourseDirectory.release_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'release'
    The name of the directory that contains the version of the assignment that
    will be released to students. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step`
    variable in the `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.root=<Unicode>
    Default: ''
    The root directory for the course files (that includes the `source`,
    `release`, `submitted`, `autograded`, etc. directories). Defaults to the
    current working directory.
--CourseDirectory.source_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'source'
    The name of the directory that contains the master/instructor version of
    assignments. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in the
    `directory_structure` config option.
--CourseDirectory.student_id=<Unicode>
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match student IDs. This can be changed to filter by student.
    Note: this is always changed to '.' when running `nbgrader assign`, as the
    assign step doesn't have any student ID associated with it.
    If the ID is purely numeric and you are passing it as a flag on the command
    line, you will need to escape the quotes in order to have it detected as a
    string, for example `--student=""12345""`. See:
        https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader/issues/743
    for more details.
--CourseDirectory.submitted_directory=<Unicode>
    Default: 'submitted'
    The name of the directory that contains assignments that have been submitted
    by students for grading. This corresponds to the `nbgrader_step` variable in
    the `directory_structure` config option.

BaseConverter options
---------------------
--BaseConverter.force=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Whether to overwrite existing assignments/submissions
--BaseConverter.permissions=<Int>
    Default: 0
    Permissions to set on files output by nbgrader. The default is generally
    read-only (444), with the exception of nbgrader assign and nbgrader
    feedback, in which case the user also has write permission.

Assign options
--------------
--Assign.create_assignment=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Whether to create the assignment at runtime if it does not already exist.
--Assign.force=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Whether to overwrite existing assignments/submissions
--Assign.no_database=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Do not save information about the assignment into the database.
--Assign.permissions=<Int>
    Default: 0
    Permissions to set on files output by nbgrader. The default is generally
    read-only (444), with the exception of nbgrader assign and nbgrader
    feedback, in which case the user also has write permission.

Examples
--------

    Produce the version of the assignment that is intended to be released to
    students. This performs several modifications to the original assignment:

        1. It inserts a header and/or footer to each notebook in the
           assignment, if the header/footer are specified.

        2. It locks certain cells so that they cannot be deleted by students
           accidentally (or on purpose!)

        3. It removes solutions from the notebooks and replaces them with
           code or text stubs saying (for example) "YOUR ANSWER HERE".

        4. It clears all outputs from the cells of the notebooks.

        5. It saves information about the cell contents so that we can warn
           students if they have changed the tests, or if they have failed
           to provide a response to a written answer. Specifically, this is
           done by computing a checksum of the cell contents and saving it
           into the cell metadata.

        6. It saves the tests used to grade students' code into a database,
           so that those tests can be replaced during autograding if they
           were modified by the student (you can prevent this by passing the
           --no-db flag).

           Additionally, the assignment must already be present in the
           database. To create it while running `nbgrader assign` if it
           doesn't already exist, pass the --create flag.

    `nbgrader assign` takes one argument (the name of the assignment), and
    looks for notebooks in the 'source' directory by default, according to
    the directory structure specified in `CourseDirectory.directory_structure`.
    The student version is then saved into the 'release' directory.

    Note that the directory structure requires the `student_id` to be given;
    however, there is no student ID at this point in the process. Instead,
    `nbgrader assign` sets the student ID to be '.' so by default, files are
    read in according to:

        source/./{assignment_id}/{notebook_id}.ipynb

    and saved according to:

        release/./{assignment_id}/{notebook_id}.ipynb

PASSED

Options
-------

Arguments that take values are actually convenience aliases to full
Configurables, whose aliases are listed on the help line. For more information
on full configurables, see '--help-all'.

--debug
    set log level to DEBUG (maximize logging output)
--quiet
    set log level to CRITICAL (minimize logging output)
--create
    Create an entry for the student in the database, if one does not already exist.
--no-execute
    Don't execute notebooks and clear output when autograding.
--force
    Overwrite an assignment/submission if it already exists.
-f
    Overwrite an assignment/submission if it already exists.
--log-level=<Enum> (Application.log_level)
    Default: 30
    Choices: (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 'DEBUG', 'INFO', 'WARN', 'ERROR', 'CRITICAL')
    Set the log level by value or name.
--student=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.student_id)
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match student IDs. This can be changed to filter by student.
    Note: this is always changed to '.' when running `nbgrader assign`, as the
    assign step doesn't have any student ID associated with it.
    If the ID is purely numeric and you are passing it as a flag on the command
    line, you will need to escape the quotes in order to have it detected as a
    string, for example `--student=""12345""`. See:
        https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader/issues/743
    for more details.
--assignment=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.assignment_id)
    Default: ''
    The assignment name. This MUST be specified, either by setting the config
    option, passing an argument on the command line, or using the --assignment
    option on the command line.
--notebook=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.notebook_id)
    Default: '*'
    File glob to match notebook names, excluding the '.ipynb' extension. This
    can be changed to filter by notebook.
--db=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.db_url)
    Default: ''
    URL to the database. Defaults to sqlite:///<root>/gradebook.db, where <root>
    is another configurable variable.
--course-dir=<Unicode> (CourseDirectory.root)
    Default: ''
    The root directory for the course files (that includes the `source`,
    `release`, `submitted`, `autograded`, etc. directories). Defaults to the
    current working directory.

BaseConverter options
---------------------
--BaseConverter.force=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Whether to overwrite existing assignments/submissions
--BaseConverter.permissions=<Int>
    Default: 0
    Permissions to set on files output by nbgrader. The default is generally
    read-only (444), with the exception of nbgrader assign and nbgrader
    feedback, in which case the user also has write permission.

Autograde options
-----------------
--Autograde.create_student=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Whether to create the student at runtime if it does not already exist.
--Autograde.exclude_overwriting=<Dict>
    Default: {}
    A dictionary with keys corresponding to assignment names and values being a
    list of filenames (relative to the assignment's source directory) that
    should NOT be overwritten with the source version. This is to allow students
    to e.g. edit a python file and submit it alongside the notebooks in their
    assignment.
--Autograde.force=<Bool>
    Default: False
    Whether to overwrite existing assignments/submissions
--Autograde.permissions=<Int>
    Default: 0
    Permissions to set on files output by nbgrader. The default is generally
    read-only (444), with the exception of nbgrader assign and nbgrader
    feedback, in which case the user also has write permission.

Examples
--------

    Autograde submitted assignments. This takes one argument for the
    assignment id, and then (by default) autogrades assignments from the
    following directory structure:

        submitted/*/{assignment_id}/*.ipynb

    and saves the autograded files to the corresponding directory in:

        autograded/{student_id}/{assignment_id}/{notebook_id}.ipynb

    The student IDs must already exist in the database. If they do not, you
    can tell `nbgrader autograde` to add them on the fly by passing the
    --create flag.

    Note that the assignment must also be present in the database. If it is
    not, you should first create it using `nbgrader assign`. Then, during
    autograding, the cells that contain tests for the students' answers will
    be overwritten with the master version of the tests that is saved in the
    database (this prevents students from modifying the tests in order to
    improve their score).

    To grade all submissions for "Problem Set 1":

        nbgrader autograde "Problem Set 1"

    To grade only the submission by student with ID 'Hacker':

        nbgrader autograde "Problem Set 1" --student Hacker

    To grade only the notebooks that start with '1':

        nbgrader autograde "Problem Set 1" --notebook "1*"

    By default, student submissions are re-executed and their output cleared.
    For long running notebooks, it can be useful to disable this with the
    '--no-execute' flag:

        nbgrader autograde "Problem Set 1" --no-execute

    Note, however, that doing so will not guarantee that students' solutions
    are correct. If you use this flag, you should make sure you manually
    check all solutions. For example, if a student saved their notebook with
    all outputs cleared, then using --no-execute would result in them
    receiving full credit on all autograded problems.
